the only non-trivial case is when \(y=\jmath_\bot(z)\) and
\(y'=\jmath_\bot(z')\) with \(l^\bot(z)=l^\bot(z')\). In that case,
\[
j_2(y)
=
j_1(l^\bot(z))
=
j_1(l^\bot(z'))
=
j_2(y'),
\]
so the value of \(w\) is independent of the chosen preimage.

Since \(j_1\) and \(j_2\) are homomorphisms and agree on the overlap,
\(w\) is a homomorphism. By construction,
\[
\jmath_{\mathcal F}\fatsemi w=j_1
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
l^{\bot}_{\join}\fatsemi w=j_2.
\]
The two images jointly cover \(\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}\), so these
equations also make \(w\) unique.
The upper square is a pushout by the original EDPOI square, and by the pasting property, the whole rectangle is a pushout.
By construction, $m^{\join}$ is anchored and therefore we successfully checked condition (1) of~\cref{def:extended_complement}.

We next verify condition~(2). Let
\[
e\in E_{\mathcal L},
\qquad
e\neq\top_{\mathcal L},
\]
and let
\[
x\in
V_{\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}}
\coprod
E_{\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}}
\]
satisfy
\[
(m^{\join})_{E}(e)
<^\mu_{\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}}
x.
\]
We must show that \(x\in\operatorname{im}(m^{\join})\).
By construction, $x$ has a preimage in $\mathcal{F}$, say $x = \jmath_{\mathcal{F}}(x')$, and $\jmath_{\mathcal{F}}$ reflects the immediate predecessor relation.
Therefore, $m_{E}(e) <^{\mu}_{\mathcal{F}} x'$ and by condition (2) of the original extended pushout complement, $x' \in \operatorname{im}(m)$ and hence $x = \jmath_{\mathcal{F}}(x') \in \operatorname{im}(m^{\join})$.

By construction, \(\jmath_{\mathcal F}\) is injective on vertices and
edges, while \(m\) is injective on the contents of each
\(\boxsort\)-edge of \(\mathcal L\).
Thus \(m^{\join} = m \fatsemi \jmath_{\mathcal F}\) is injective on the
contents of each \(\boxsort\)-edge of \(\mathcal L\), establishing
condition~(3).

We now check condition~(4).
Let
\[
N_{\mathcal F}
=
S\coprod n^1\coprod\cdots\coprod n'\coprod\cdots\coprod n^p
\]
and
\[
K_{\mathcal F}
=
T\coprod k^1\coprod\cdots\coprod k'\coprod\cdots\coprod k^p
\]
be the internal interfaces of \(\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}\), according
to~\cref{def:join_of_extended_cospans}.

Write
\[
S_{\mathrm{in}}\defeq\iota_n(S_n)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
S_{\mathrm{out}}\defeq\iota_k(S_k)
\]
for the affected subinterfaces of \(N_{\mathcal F}\) and
\(K_{\mathcal F}\), where \(\iota_n\) and \(\iota_k\) are the
coprojections of the distinguished interface blocks. The transported
match \(m^{\join}\) affects only the distinguished component
\(\mathcal F\). Moreover, since \(\alpha_n\) and \(\alpha_k\) are
canonical block isomorphisms, \(S_{\mathrm{in}}\) and
\(S_{\mathrm{out}}\) are unions of complete interface blocks.
Therefore, by our convention on interface objects,
\[
\begin{aligned}
N_{\bot}
\defeq
N_{\mathcal F}\setminus S_{\mathrm{in}}
&\cong
S\coprod n^1\coprod\cdots
\coprod(n'\setminus S_n)
\coprod\cdots\coprod n^p,\\
K_{\bot}
\defeq
K_{\mathcal F}\setminus S_{\mathrm{out}}
&\cong
T\coprod k^1\coprod\cdots
\coprod(k'\setminus S_k)
\coprod\cdots\coprod k^p.
\end{aligned}
\]
This makes
\[
n
\xrightarrow{\mathcal J^n_{\bot,\mathrm{ext}}}
N_{\bot}
\xrightarrow{\mathcal J^n_{\bot,\mathrm{int}}}
\mathcal J_{\bot}
\xleftarrow{\mathcal J^k_{\bot,\mathrm{int}}}
K_{\bot}
\xleftarrow{\mathcal J^k_{\bot,\mathrm{ext}}}
k
\]
a well-typed extended cospan, since every component is well-typed.

Let
\[
\xi_n:N_{\bot}\hookrightarrow N_{\mathcal F}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\xi_k:K_{\bot}\hookrightarrow K_{\mathcal F}
\]
be the evident blockwise inclusions, and let
\[
n
\xrightarrow{\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{ext}}}
N_{\mathcal F}
\xrightarrow{\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}}}
\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}
\xleftarrow{\mathcal J^k_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}}}
K_{\mathcal F}
\xleftarrow{\mathcal J^k_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{ext}}}
k
\]
be the corresponding cospan for \(\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}\).

For the input interfaces, condition~(4) requires
\[
\mathcal J^n_{\bot,\mathrm{ext}}\fatsemi\xi_n
=
\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{ext}}
\]
and
\[
\mathcal J^n_{\bot,\mathrm{int}}\fatsemi l^\bot_{\join}
=
\xi_n\fatsemi\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}}.
\]
The first equation is immediate: both sides are the coprojection of
the unchanged external-interface block \(S\) into
\(N_{\mathcal F}\).

The second equation may be checked on the interface blocks. On \(S\)
and on every block \(n^q\neq n'\), the maps
\(\mathcal J^n_{\bot,\mathrm{int}}\) and
\(\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}}\) agree, while \(\xi_n\)
and \(l^\bot_{\join}\) act as the identity on the corresponding
blocks and their images.

On the retained distinguished block \(n'\setminus S_n\), we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\left(
\mathcal J^n_{\bot,\mathrm{int}}
\fatsemi l^\bot_{\join}
\right)
\restriction_{n'\setminus S_n}
\\
&\qquad =
g_n^\bot
\fatsemi\jmath_{\bot}
\fatsemi l^\bot_{\join}
\\
&\qquad =
g_n^\bot
\fatsemi l^\bot
\fatsemi\jmath_{\mathcal F}
\\
&\qquad =
\jmath_n
\fatsemi g_{\mathrm{int}}
\fatsemi\jmath_{\mathcal F}
\\
&\qquad =
\left(
\xi_n
\fatsemi\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}}
\right)
\restriction_{n'\setminus S_n}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Here the second equality follows from
\[
\jmath_{\bot}\fatsemi l^\bot_{\join}
=
l^\bot\fatsemi\jmath_{\mathcal F},
\]
and the third is condition~(4) for the original extended pushout
complement. The two composites also agree on the root by strictness.
Thus
\[
\mathcal J^n_{\bot,\mathrm{int}}\fatsemi l^\bot_{\join}
=
\xi_n\fatsemi\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}}.
\]

The output-interface equations follow by the same blockwise argument,
using \(g_k^\bot\), \(\jmath_k\), and \(\xi_k\). Hence condition~(4)
holds.

It remains to check condition~(5).
The transported match \(m\fatsemi\jmath_{\mathcal F}\) has anchor
\[
a'\defeq\jmath_{\mathcal F,E}(a).
\]
Recall that the affected subinterfaces of \(\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}\)
are
\[
S_{\mathrm{in}}=\iota_n(S_n)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
S_{\mathrm{out}}=\iota_k(S_k),
\]
where \(\iota_n\) and \(\iota_k\) denote the corestrictions of the
corresponding coprojections to these affected blocks. The maps required
by condition~(5) are therefore
\[
\alpha_n\fatsemi\iota_n:
i'\setminus i\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{in}}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\alpha_k\fatsemi\iota_k:
j'\setminus j\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{out}}.
\]
Since \(\alpha_n\) and \(\alpha_k\) are canonical block
isomorphisms, and \(\iota_n\) and \(\iota_k\) are isomorphisms onto
the corresponding affected blocks, these composites are canonical
isomorphisms of pointed discrete interfaces.

Define the re-anchored interface maps
\[
\widehat{(\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}})}^{\,a'}
\defeq
\left\langle
a';
\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int},V}
\restriction_{V_{S_{\mathrm{in}}}}
\right\rangle
:
S_{\mathrm{in}}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}
\]
and
\[
\widehat{(\mathcal J^k_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}})}^{\,a'}
\defeq
\left\langle
a';
\mathcal J^k_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int},V}
\restriction_{V_{S_{\mathrm{out}}}}
\right\rangle
:
S_{\mathrm{out}}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}.
\]
It is sufficient to show that the following pasted diagrams commute:
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
	{i'\setminus i} && {\mathcal L} \\
	\\
	{S_n} && {\mathcal F} \\
	\\
	{S_{\mathrm{in}}} && {\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}}
	\arrow["{f_{\mathrm{int}}\restriction}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\alpha_n}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["m", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\widehat g_n^{\,a}}"', from=3-1, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\iota_n}"', from=3-1, to=5-1]
	\arrow["{\jmath_{\mathcal F}}"', from=3-3, to=5-3]
	\arrow[
	  "{\widehat{(\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}})}^{\,a'}}",
	  from=5-1,
	  to=5-3
	]
\end{tikzcd}
\qquad
\begin{tikzcd}
	{j'\setminus j} && {\mathcal L} \\
	\\
	{S_k} && {\mathcal F} \\
	\\
	{S_{\mathrm{out}}} && {\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}}
	\arrow["{f_{\mathrm{int}}\restriction}", from=1-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow["{\alpha_k}"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow["m", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\widehat g_k^{\,a}}"', from=3-1, to=3-3]
	\arrow["{\iota_k}"', from=3-1, to=5-1]
	\arrow["{\jmath_{\mathcal F}}"', from=3-3, to=5-3]
	\arrow[
	  "{\widehat{(\mathcal J^k_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}})}^{\,a'}}",
	  from=5-1,
	  to=5-3
	]
\end{tikzcd}
\]
We check the left diagram; the right diagram is analogous. The upper
square commutes by condition~(5) for the original extended pushout
complement. It remains to check the lower square.

On the root, both composites have value \(a'\), since
\[
\jmath_{\mathcal F,E}
\bigl(
\widehat g_{n,E}^{\,a}(\top_{S_n})
\bigr)
=
\jmath_{\mathcal F,E}(a)
=
a'
\]
and
\[
\widehat{(\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}})}^{\,a'}_E
\bigl(
\iota_{n,E}(\top_{S_n})
\bigr)
=
a'.
\]
For every \(x\in V_{S_n}\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat{(\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int}})}^{\,a'}_V
\bigl(\iota_{n,V}(x)\bigr)
&=
\mathcal J^n_{\mathcal F,\mathrm{int},V}
\bigl(\iota_{n,V}(x)\bigr)
\\
&=
\jmath_{\mathcal F,V}
\bigl(g_{\mathrm{int},V}(x)\bigr)
\\
&=
\jmath_{\mathcal F,V}
\bigl(\widehat g_{n,V}^{\,a}(x)\bigr).
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus the lower square, and hence the whole left rectangle, commutes.
The right rectangle follows by the same argument. This proves
condition~(5).

Thus, we have proved that
\[
i\coprod j
\xrightarrow{c^{\join}}
\mathcal J_{\bot}
\xrightarrow{l^\bot_{\join}}
\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}
\]
is an extended pushout complement.

It remains to construct the second pushout and identify the resulting
extended cospan with \(\mathcal J_{\mathcal G}\).

Let
\[
\jmath_{\mathcal G}:
\mathcal G\longrightarrow\mathcal J_{\mathcal G}
\]
denote the inclusion of the distinguished component. Choosing the fresh
join structure compatibly, define
\[
\widetilde{\iota_{\bot}}^{\join}:
\mathcal J_{\bot}\longrightarrow\mathcal J_{\mathcal G}
\]
to act as
\(\widetilde{\iota_{\bot}}:\mathcal L^\bot\to\mathcal G\)
on the distinguished component and as the identity on the fresh join
structure and every other component. In particular,
\[
\jmath_{\bot}\fatsemi
\widetilde{\iota_{\bot}}^{\join}
=
\widetilde{\iota_{\bot}}\fatsemi
\jmath_{\mathcal G}.
\]

Consider the commutative diagram
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
	{i\coprod j} & {\mathcal R} \\
	{\mathcal L^\bot} & {\mathcal G} \\
	{\mathcal J_{\bot}} & {\mathcal J_{\mathcal G}}
	\arrow["p", from=1-1, to=1-2]
	\arrow["c"', from=1-1, to=2-1]
	\arrow["{\widetilde{\iota_{\mathcal R}}}", from=1-2, to=2-2]
	\arrow["{\widetilde{\iota_{\bot}}}"', from=2-1, to=2-2]
	\arrow["{\jmath_{\bot}}"', from=2-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow[
	  "\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=180},
	  draw=none,
	  from=2-2,
	  to=1-1
	]
	\arrow["{\jmath_{\mathcal G}}", from=2-2, to=3-2]
	\arrow[
	  "{\widetilde{\iota_{\bot}}^{\join}}"',
	  from=3-1,
	  to=3-2
	]
	\arrow[
	  "\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=180},
	  draw=none,
	  from=3-2,
	  to=2-1
	]
\end{tikzcd}
\]
The upper square is the second pushout square of the original EDPOI
rewrite. The lower square is a pushout by the same universal-property
argument used for the first pushout square. Therefore, the whole
rectangle is a pushout.

By definition, the extended cospan for \(\mathcal J_{\mathcal G}\) has
internal interfaces
\[
N_{\mathcal G}
=
S\coprod n^1\coprod\cdots\coprod n''\coprod\cdots\coprod n^p
\]
and
\[
K_{\mathcal G}
=
T\coprod k^1\coprod\cdots\coprod k''\coprod\cdots\coprod k^p,
\]
with the evident interface maps. The EDPOI construction, on the other
hand, gives the internal interfaces
\[
N'_{\mathcal G}
=
N_{\bot}\coprod i''_\circ
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
K'_{\mathcal G}
=
K_{\bot}\coprod j''_\circ.
\]
Since
\[
n''
=
(n'\setminus S_n)\coprod i''_\circ
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
k''
=
(k'\setminus S_k)\coprod j''_\circ,
\]
our convention on interface objects gives canonical block
isomorphisms
\[
\beta_n:
N'_{\mathcal G}\longrightarrow N_{\mathcal G}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\beta_k:
K'_{\mathcal G}\longrightarrow K_{\mathcal G}.
\]
They are the identity on the fresh join blocks and on every unaffected
component block, and reassociate the newly introduced blocks
\(i''_\circ\) and \(j''_\circ\) into the distinguished component.
Consequently, they are precisely the interface isomorphisms permitted
by our convention.

The external legs commute with \(\beta_n\) and \(\beta_k\) because
these isomorphisms are the identity on the fresh external-interface
blocks \(S\) and \(T\). For the internal legs, compatibility holds on
the fresh join structure and the unaffected components by construction.
On the retained parts of the distinguished component, it follows from
\[
h_n\restriction_{n'\setminus S_n}
=
g_n^\bot\fatsemi\widetilde{\iota_\bot},
\qquad
h_k\restriction_{k'\setminus S_k}
=
g_k^\bot\fatsemi\widetilde{\iota_\bot},
\]
and on the newly introduced parts it follows from
\[
h_n\restriction_{i''_\circ}
=
\rho_i,
\qquad
h_k\restriction_{j''_\circ}
=
\rho_j.
\]
The maps also agree on the roots by strictness. Therefore
\(\beta_n\), the identity on \(\mathcal J_{\mathcal G}\), and
\(\beta_k\) define an isomorphism of extended cospans.

Thus, we conclude that
\[
\mathcal J_{\mathcal F}
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathfrak S}\;
\mathcal J_{\mathcal G}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\subsection{The quotient semilattice-enriched PROP}

\begin{definition}[Quotient by rewrites]
\label{def:quotient_by_rewrites}
The category
\(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R\)
has the same objects as
\(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\), and its morphisms
\(n\to m\) are the \(\sim_{\mathfrak R}\)-equivalence classes of
morphisms \(n\to m\) in
\(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\).

For morphisms
\(\mathcal F:n\to m\) and \(\mathcal G:m\to k\), whenever they are composable, define
\[
[\mathcal F]\fatsemi[\mathcal G]
\defeq
[\mathcal F\fatsemi\mathcal G],
\]
additionally, define
\[
[\mathcal F]\otimes[\mathcal G]
\defeq
[\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal G],
\qquad
\id_n\defeq[\id_n].
\]
For parallel morphisms \(\mathcal F,\mathcal G:n\to m\), define
\[
[\mathcal F]\join[\mathcal G]
\defeq
[\mathcal F\join\mathcal G].
\]
These operations are well defined by
\cref{lemma:rewriting_congruence}.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:quotient_is_slat}
The quotient $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma) / \mathfrak{R}$ is an $\catname{SLat}$-PROP.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The objects of $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma) / \mathfrak{R}$ are the same as those of $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$, which are the natural numbers.
The hom-sets $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma) / \mathfrak{R}(n, m)$ are then equipped with a $\join$ as per~\cref{def:join_of_extended_cospans}, which is associative and idempotent by~\cref{def:structural_rules} and commutative since isomorphism of extended cospans subsumes commutatitivity of $\join$ (~\cref{remark:commutativity_absorbed}).
This $\join$ further distributes over $\otimes$ and $\fatsemi$ by the rules of~\cref{def:structural_rules}, noting that connecting a $\boxsort$-edge to some other sub-hypergraph corresponds to a composition and placing it on a side corresponds to a tensor product of cospans.
Finally, $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma) / \mathfrak{R}$ inherits composition and symmetric monoidal structure from $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$, which is respected by the quotient by~\cref{lemma:rewriting_congruence}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
We write
\[
Q:
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)
\longrightarrow
\widetilde U_{\catname{SLat}}
\bigl(
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R
\bigr),
\qquad
\mathcal G\longmapsto[\mathcal G],
\]
for the quotient functor.
\end{remark}

Thus we equipped the category of extended cospans with a structure of $\catname{SLat}$-PROP.
The next step is to observe that the category $\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)$ embeds faithfully into $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:embedding_hypi}
There is a faithful identity-on-objects strict symmetric monoidal functor
\[
\Xi:
\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)
\longrightarrow
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma).
\]
Moreover, its image consists precisely of the box-free
morphisms of \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
On objects, put $\Xi(n)\defeq n$. Given a cospan
\[
n\xrightarrow{f}F\xleftarrow{g}m
\]
representing a morphism of $\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)$, let $\mathcal F$
be the rooted e-hypergraph obtained from $F$ by adjoining a fresh edge
$\top_{\mathcal F}$, with
\[
s(\top_{\mathcal F})
=
t(\top_{\mathcal F})
=
\varepsilon,
\qquad
l(\top_{\mathcal F})=\cmpsort,
\]
and declaring
\[
\top_{\mathcal F}<^\mu_{\mathcal F}x
\qquad
\text{for every }x\in V_F\coprod E_F.
\]
Extend $f$ and $g$ to the roots by sending the roots of their domains
to $\top_{\mathcal F}$. Define $\Xi$ on morphisms by
\[
\Xi
\left(
n\xrightarrow{f}F\xleftarrow{g}m
\right)
\defeq
\left(
n\xrightarrow{\id_n}n\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal F
\xleftarrow{g}m\xleftarrow{\id_m}m
\right).
\]
This is a well-typed extended cospan: its external legs are monic, its
composite interface legs are strict, and it has no strictly internal
interface vertices. An isomorphism of ordinary hypergraph cospans
extends uniquely to an isomorphism of the associated rooted extended
cospans by sending root to root, so this assignment is well defined on
isomorphism classes.

The identity cospan is sent to the identity extended cospan. Moreover,
the rooted pushout used to compose two cospans in the image of $\Xi$ is
obtained from their ordinary hypergraph pushout by adjoining a common
root. Hence $\Xi$ preserves composition.

Likewise, the rooted pushout of $\mathcal F$ and $\mathcal G$ over
their roots is obtained by adjoining a common root to the ordinary
coproduct $F\coprod G$. Thus $\Xi$ preserves tensor products, the
monoidal unit, and the symmetries, and is therefore strict symmetric
monoidal.

Let 
\[
n \xrightarrow{} n \xrightarrow{} \Xi(F) \xleftarrow{} m \xleftarrow{} m~,
\]
and
\[
n \xrightarrow{} n \xrightarrow{} \Xi(G) \xleftarrow{} m \xleftarrow{} m~,
\]
be two isomorphic cospans.
Then there is an isomorphism $\phi : \Xi(F) \to \Xi(G)$ which reduces to the isomorphism of underlying hypergraphs $F$ and $G$ and therefore
\[
n \xrightarrow{} F \xleftarrow{} m \qquad \text{and} \qquad n \xrightarrow{} G \xleftarrow{} m
\]
are isomorphic in $\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)$ and the functor is faithful.

Finally, the functor is full when corestricted to box-free extended cospans in $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$.
Consider an extended cospan
\[
n \xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{ext}}} n' \xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{int}}} \mathcal{F} \xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{int}}} m' \xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{ext}}} m~,
\]
such that $\mathcal{F}$ has no $\boxsort$-edges and thus its only $\cmpsort$-edge is $\top$.
The condition on the strictly internal interfaces in~\cref{def:extended_cospan} therefore implies that the images of $f_{\mathrm{int},V}(V_{n'} \setminus f_{\mathrm{ext},V}(V_n)) = \varnothing$ and $g_{\mathrm{int},V}(V_{m'} \setminus g_{\mathrm{ext},V}(V_m)) = \varnothing$ and thus
$V_{n'} \setminus f_{\mathrm{ext},V}(V_n) = \varnothing$ and $V_{m'} \setminus g_{\mathrm{ext},V}(V_m) = \varnothing$, so $n' \cong n$ and $m' \cong m$ with $f_{\mathrm{ext}}$ and $g_{\mathrm{ext}}$ being isomorphisms.
Let \(F\) be the ordinary hypergraph obtained from \(\mathcal F\) by
deleting \(\top_{\mathcal F}\) and forgetting the hierarchical
relation. The vertex components of the strict composites determine
the ordinary hypergraph cospan
\[
n
\xrightarrow{
  (f_{\mathrm{ext}}\fatsemi f_{\mathrm{int}})_V
}
F
\xleftarrow{
  (g_{\mathrm{ext}}\fatsemi g_{\mathrm{int}})_V
}
m.
\]
Applying \(\Xi\) to this cospan gives an extended cospan isomorphic to
the original one: the internal-interface isomorphisms are
\(f_{\mathrm{ext}}\) and \(g_{\mathrm{ext}}\), and the carrier
isomorphism identifies the newly adjoined root with
\(\top_{\mathcal F}\). Hence every box-free morphism lies in the image of \(\Xi\).
\end{proof}

We will then show that each morphism in $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma) / \mathfrak{R}$ is essentially a collection of morphisms in $\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)$, so that our extended cospan representation is full in a certain sense.
We will see that each $\sim_{\mathfrak{R}}$-equivalence class of morphisms in $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$ has a canonical representative.
To show that such representative is unique, we need some invariant that is shared by all members of a $\sim_{\mathfrak{R}}$-equivalence class.
Such an invariant is given by the set of complete box-free resolutions. 
We define this invariant next.

\subsection{Canonical representatives}

\begin{definition}[Components and flat resolutions]
\label{def:flat_resolutions}
Let $\mathbf G:n\to m$ be the well-typed extended cospan
\[
\mathbf G
=
\left(
n\xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{ext}}}n'
\xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{int}}}\mathcal G
\xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{int}}}m'
\xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{ext}}}m
\right),
\]
and let $b$ be a $\boxsort$-edge of its carrier. Write
\[
\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal G}(b)
\defeq
\left\{
c\in E_{\mathcal G,\cmpsortsubscript}
\;\middle|\;
b<^\mu_{\mathcal G}c
\right\}~,
\]
for the set of components of $b$.

For $c\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal G}(b)$, let
$\mathcal G_c$ be the sub-e-hypergraph consisting of $c$ and all its
descendants, rooted at $c$. Let $I_c$ be the pointed discrete
interface with
\[
V_{I_c}
\defeq
\left\{
x\in V_{n'}
\;\middle|\;
c <_{\mathcal G}f_{\mathrm{int},V}(x)
\right\},
\]
and define $O_c$ dually using $g_{\mathrm{int}}$. 

The sets
\[
B_c^n
\defeq
\pi_f^{-1}(c),
\qquad
B_c^m
\defeq
\pi_g^{-1}(c)
\]
are the top input and output blocks of the component cospan. By
well-typedness,
\[
|B_c^n|=|s(b)|,
\qquad
|B_c^m|=|t(b)|.
\]
Their orders therefore determine monomorphisms
\[
\lambda_c^n:D[|s(b)|]\longrightarrow I_c,
\qquad
\lambda_c^m:D[|t(b)|]\longrightarrow O_c
\]
whose images are $B_c^n$ and $B_c^m$, respectively.

The \emph{component cospan} determined by $c$ is
\[
\mathbf G_c
\defeq
\left(
D[|s(b)|]
\xrightarrow{\lambda_c^n}
I_c
\xrightarrow{
\left\langle
c;
f_{\mathrm{int},V}\restriction_{V_{I_c}}
\right\rangle}
\mathcal G_c
\xleftarrow{
\left\langle
c;
g_{\mathrm{int},V}\restriction_{V_{O_c}}
\right\rangle}
O_c
\xleftarrow{\lambda_c^m}
D[|t(b)|]
\right),
\]
which is a well-typed extended cospan.

Put
\[
\mathbf B_b
\defeq
\bigjoin_{d\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal G}(b)}
\mathbf G_d,
\]
and write \(\mathcal B_b\) for its carrier. 
Both \(\mathbf B_b\) and
\(\mathbf G_c\), for \(c\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal G}(b)\), have
external interfaces
\[
D[|s(b)|]
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
D[|t(b)|].
\]

Let
\[
c_0\defeq {<^\mu_{\mathcal G}}(b).
\]
There is a canonical anchored match
\[
m_b:\mathcal B_b\longrightarrow\mathcal G
\]
which sends the fresh root of \(\mathcal B_b\) to \(c_0\), its fresh
\(\boxsort\)-edge to \(b\), its source and target vertices to \(s(b)\)
and \(t(b)\) in order, and each copied component
\(\mathcal G_d\) into the corresponding component of \(b\).

For \(c\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal G}(b)\), the
\emph{resolution of \(b\) at \(c\)}, denoted
\[
\mathbf G[b\mapsto c],
\]
is the result of applying at \(m_b\) the EDPOI rule whose left-hand side
is \(\mathbf B_b\) and whose right-hand side is \(\mathbf G_c\).
It can be checked that the match satisfies all the conditions of~\cref{def:extended_complement}, and since the maps $D[|s(b)|] \coprod D([|t(b)|] \xrightarrow{} \mathcal{B}_{b})$ are monic, the pushout complement is unique by~\cref{lemma:complement_unique}.

A \emph{complete resolution} of \(\mathbf G\) is a sequence
\[
\mathbf G=\mathbf G_0,\mathbf G_1,\ldots,\mathbf G_r
\]
such that, for every \(0\leq q<r\),
\[
\mathbf G_{q+1}
=
\mathbf G_q[b_q\mapsto c_q]
\]
for some \(\boxsort\)-edge \(b_q\) of \(\mathbf G_q\) and some
\[
c_q\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal G_q}(b_q),
\]
and such that \(\mathbf G_r\) contains no \(\boxsort\)-edges. Each
resolution removes at least the selected edge \(b_q\), so every
sequence of resolutions terminates.

Every complete resolution is box-free and hence, by
\cref{prop:embedding_hypi}, is isomorphic to \(\Xi(F)\) for a unique
\[
F\in\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)(n,m).
\]
We define
\[
\operatorname{Flat}(\mathbf G)
\defeq
\left\{
F\in\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)(n,m)
\;\middle|\;
\begin{array}{c}
\text{some complete resolution of \(\mathbf G\)}\\
\text{is isomorphic to \(\Xi(F)\)}
\end{array}
\right\}.
\]
\end{definition}

Note that the rules $\langle \mathbf{B}_b, \mathbf{G}_{c} \rangle$ are not members of $\mathfrak{R}$.
They are simply a method of defining the set of complete resolutions in a primitive way.

\begin{example}
The EDPOI diagram below gives an example of a complete resolution:
\[
\adjustbox{scale=0.75}{
  \tikzfig{./figures/monoidal_egraphs/flattening_example_1}~.
}
\]
\end{example}

% \begin{proposition}
% \label{prop:flattening}
% For well-typed extended cospans of appropriate types,
% \[
% \operatorname{Flat}(\Xi(F))=\{F\},
% \]
% \[
% \operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal F\join\mathcal G)
% =
% \operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal F)
% \cup
% \operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal G),
% \]
% \[
% \operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal F\fatsemi\mathcal G)
% =
% \left\{
% F\fatsemi
% \;\middle|\;
% F\in\operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal F),\
% G\in\operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal G)
% \right\},
% \]
% and
% \[
% \operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal G)
% =
% \left\{
% F\otimes G
% \;\middle|\;
% F\in\operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal F),\
% G\in\operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal G)
% \right\}.
% \]
% \end{proposition}
% \begin{proof}
% The first equality follows because \(\Xi(F)\) is box-free and its
% underlying ordinary cospan is \(F\).
% The other equalities follow by the definition of $\operatorname{Flat}$ and the fact that $\boxsort$-edges do not span across composition and tensor products of cospans.
% \end{proof}

With this, we are ready to show that every $\sim_{\mathfrak{R}}$-equivalence class of morphisms in $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$ has a canonical representative.
Intuitively, such a representative is obtained by moving vertices and edges inside $\boxsort$-edges and flatten nested such edges and then picking a canonical external interfaces.

\begin{lemma}[Canonical representative]\label{lemma:canonical_representative}
  Every well-typed extended cospan $\mathcal{G}$ can be turned under $\mathfrak{R}_\downarrow$ into either of the following forms:
  \begin{enumerate}
  \item A well-typed extended cospan $\Xi(F)$ for some $F \in \catname{HypI}(\Sigma)$,
  \item A well-typed extended cospan  $\Xi(F_1) \join \cdots \join \Xi(F_n)$ for $F_i$ in $\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)$ and for each $i \not = j$, $\Xi(F_i) \not \cong \Xi(F_j)$.
  \end{enumerate}
  Moreover, such forms are unique up to isomorphism of extended cospans.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Suppose $\mathcal{G}$ has no $\boxsort$-edges, then it is readily of the form (1), so we will consider the case when $\mathcal{G}$ has at least one $\boxsort$-edge.
Start with the level $1$ of the hierarchy of $\mathcal{G}$, i.e., with those vertices and edges that have depth $1$ (~\cref{def:depth}).
We apply rewrite rules corresponding to the two equations in the first row of~\cref{fig:string-equations} as long as the $f$ or $g$ part of these rules contain at least one edge---then this process terminates because the number of edges at level $1$ reduces.
Then apply rewrite rules corresponding to the two equations in the second row of~\cref{fig:string-equations}; this again terminates as the number of edges and vertices at this level reduces.
This step turns $\mathcal{G}$ into a well-typed extended cospan of the form
\[
n \xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{ext}}} n' \xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{int}}} \mathcal{G}' \xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{int}}} m' \xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{ext}}} m~,
\]
where $\mathcal{G}'$ has no edges or vertices at level $1$ other than a single $\boxsort$-edge $b$ and vertices $s(b)$ and $t(b)$.
We repeat the same process recursively for each component of $b$.
This process terminates when a $\boxsort$-edge at level $l$ has no decsendant $\boxsort$-edges.
Consider such a $\boxsort$ edge $b'$ for a largest such $l$.
Its nearest ancestor $\boxsort$-edge $b''$ at level $l-2$ has as components: a component $c$ containing $b'$ and other components $c_i$ such that they either contain $\boxsort$-edge with the same structure as $b'$ or contain no $\boxsort$-edges. 
We can then apply the rewrite rule corresponding to the associativity equation in the third row of~\cref{fig:string-equations}.
We repeat this recursively bottom-up.
At this point our top-level $\boxsort$-edge $b$ has as components $c_i$ such that they do not contain any $\boxsort$-edges, so we can apply the idempotency rule to remove duplciate components.
We then apply reindexing rules corresponding to $f_{\mathrm{ext}} \fatsemi f_{\mathrm{int}}$ and $g_{\mathrm{ext}} \fatsemi g_{\mathrm{int}}$ which turns $\mathcal{G}$ into a well-typed extended cospan of the form
\[
\mathbf{G}' = \bigl( n \xrightarrow{f'_{\mathrm{ext}}} n' \xrightarrow{f'_{\mathrm{int}}} \mathcal{G}' \xleftarrow{g'_{\mathrm{int}}} m' \xleftarrow{g'_{\mathrm{ext}}} m \bigr)~,
\]
such that $\mathcal{G}'$ has a single $\boxsort$-edge $b$ such that $\top_{\mathcal{G}'} <^{\mu}_{\mathcal{G}'} b$ and contains no other $\boxsort$-edges.
The ordering on $n$ and $m$ then coincides with the ordering on $s(b)$ and $t(b)$, respectively.
This way, every component of $b$ together with its portion of strictly internal input and output interfaces represent a cospan $\Xi(F_i)$ for some $F_i \in \catname{HypI}(\Sigma)$.
By definition of $\join$, we then have that $\mathbf{G}' \cong \Xi(F_1) \join \cdots \join \Xi(F_n)$.

Finally, observe that $\sim_{\mathcal{R}}$ preserves $\operatorname{Flat}$: this can be checked by comparing $\operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal{L})$ and $\operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal{R})$ for each induced rewrite rule.
Suppose there are two well-typed extended cospans
\[
\mathbf{G}_1 = \Xi(F_1) \join \cdots \join \Xi(F_n)~, \qquad \mathbf{G}_2 = \Xi(G_1) \join \cdots \join \Xi(G_m)~,
\]
such that $\mathbf{G}_1 \sim_{\mathcal{R}} \mathbf{G}_2$.
Then we have that $\operatorname{Flat}(\mathbf{G}_1) = \operatorname{Flat}(\mathbf{G}_2)$, which implies that $n = m$ and for each $i$, there exists a unique $j$ such that $\Xi(F_i) \cong \Xi(G_j)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}
Every well-typed extended cospan $\mathcal{G}$ in $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$ satisfies
\[
[\mathcal G]
=
\bigjoin_{F\in\operatorname{Flat}(\mathcal G)}
[\Xi(F)],
\]
where $[]$ denotes the $\sim_{\mathfrak{R}}$-equivalence class given by $Q$.
\end{corollary}

We are now ready to prove one of the main results of this chapter: the
correspondence between the Frobenius $\catname{SLat}$-PROP
$\mathbf{H}_{\Sigma}^{\join}$ and
$\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak{R}$.
The proof is mostly routine, since we have already established that every
equivalence class of extended cospans has a canonical representative given
by a join of cospans $\Xi(F)$, for
$F\in\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)(n,m)$.
Moreover, by~\cref{theorem:frobenius_isomorphism}, every such $F$ is of the
form $\llangle f\rrangle$ for some
$f\in\mathbf{H}_{\Sigma}(n,m)$.
Each hom-object of $\mathbf{H}^{\join}_{\Sigma}$ is the semilattice of
finite non-empty subsets of morphisms of $\mathbf{H}_{\Sigma}$.
Since all the categories in play have the same objects, it therefore
remains to make this correspondence between their hom-objects precise.

\subsection{The presentation theorem}

\begin{theorem}\label{theorem:ehypi_slat_isomorphism}
There is an isomorphism of $\catname{SLat}$-PROPs
\[
\mathbf H_{\Sigma}^{\join}
\cong
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By~\cref{theorem:frobenius_isomorphism}, there is an isomorphism of
PROPs
\[
\llangle-\rrangle:
\mathbf H_{\Sigma}
\longrightarrow
\catname{HypI}(\Sigma).
\]
Applying the free-enrichment functor gives an isomorphism of
\(\catname{SLat}\)-PROPs
\[
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}(\llangle-\rrangle):
\mathbf H_{\Sigma}^{\join}
\longrightarrow
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}
\bigl(\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)\bigr).
\]
By~\cref{prop:quotient_is_slat},
\[
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R
\]
is also an \(\catname{SLat}\)-PROP. It therefore suffices to construct
an isomorphism
\[
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}
\bigl(\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)\bigr)
\cong
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R.
\]

Put
\[
\overline\Xi
\defeq
\Xi\fatsemi Q:
\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)
\longrightarrow
\widetilde U_{\catname{SLat}}
\bigl(
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R
\bigr).
\]
Both \(\Xi\) and \(Q\) are identity-on-objects strict symmetric
monoidal functors. Hence, by the free-enrichment adjunction of
\cref{prop:prop-adjunction}, \(\overline\Xi\) extends uniquely to an
identity-on-objects strict symmetric monoidal
\(\catname{SLat}\)-functor
\[
\Phi:
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}
\bigl(\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)\bigr)
\longrightarrow
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R.
\]

By~\cref{rem:slat-free-concrete}, a morphism of the source is a finite
non-empty subset
\[
S=\{F_1,\ldots,F_p\}
\subseteq
\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)(n,m).
\]
Since
\[
S=\{F_1\}\join\cdots\join\{F_p\},
\]
and the transpose satisfies
\[
\Phi(\{F\})
=
\overline\Xi(F)
=
Q(\Xi(F))
=
[\Xi(F)],
\]
we obtain
\[
\Phi(S)
=
\bigjoin_{F\in S}[\Xi(F)]
=
\left[\bigjoin_{F\in S}\Xi(F)\right].
\]

For every finite non-empty subset
\[
S\subseteq\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)(n,m),
\]
let \(\operatorname{Can}(S)\) denote its canonical flat-join
representative:
\[
\operatorname{Can}(S)
\defeq
\begin{cases}
\Xi(F),&S=\{F\},\\[1mm]
\displaystyle\bigjoin_{F\in S}\Xi(F),&|S|\geq2,
\end{cases}
\]
where the second line denotes the direct \(S\)-indexed join. This is
well defined up to isomorphism of extended cospans because the
components of a box carry no order. By construction,
\[
[\operatorname{Can}(S)]
=
\bigjoin_{F\in S}[\Xi(F)].
\]
Consequently,
\[
\Phi(S)=[\operatorname{Can}(S)].
\]

Define
\[
\Psi:
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R
\longrightarrow
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}
\bigl(\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)\bigr)
\]
to be the identity on objects and, on morphisms, put
\[
\Psi([\mathcal G])
\defeq
S,
\]
where \(S\) is the unique finite non-empty subset satisfying
\[
\mathcal G\sim_{\mathfrak R}\operatorname{Can}(S).
\]
Existence and uniqueness of \(S\) follow from
\cref{lemma:canonical_representative}. In particular, if
\([\mathcal G]=[\mathcal H]\), then \(\mathcal G\) and \(\mathcal H\)
belong to the same \(\sim_{\mathfrak R}\)-class and therefore have the
same canonical representative. Thus \(\Psi\) is well defined.

We verify that \(\Psi\) is an \(\catname{SLat}\)-functor. Suppose
\[
\Psi([\mathcal F])=S,
\qquad
\Psi([\mathcal G])=T.
\]
Then
\[
[\mathcal F]
=
[\operatorname{Can}(S)]
=
\bigjoin_{F\in S}[\Xi(F)]
\]
and
\[
[\mathcal G]
=
[\operatorname{Can}(T)]
=
\bigjoin_{G\in T}[\Xi(G)].
\]
The semilattice equations in the quotient therefore give
\[
\begin{aligned}
[\mathcal F]\join[\mathcal G]
&=
\left(
\bigjoin_{F\in S}[\Xi(F)]
\right)
\join
\left(
\bigjoin_{G\in T}[\Xi(G)]
\right)
\\
&=
\bigjoin_{H\in S\cup T}[\Xi(H)]
\\
&=
[\operatorname{Can}(S\cup T)].
\end{aligned}
\]
By uniqueness of canonical representatives,
\[
\Psi([\mathcal F]\join[\mathcal G])
=
S\cup T
=
\Psi([\mathcal F])\cup\Psi([\mathcal G]).
\]
Thus every hom-map of \(\Psi\) is a semilattice homomorphism.

Now suppose that the composite
\([\mathcal F]\fatsemi[\mathcal G]\) is defined. By bilinearity of
composition in the quotient,
\[
\begin{aligned}
[\mathcal F]\fatsemi[\mathcal G]
&=
\left(
\bigjoin_{F\in S}[\Xi(F)]
\right)
\fatsemi
\left(
\bigjoin_{G\in T}[\Xi(G)]
\right)
\\
&=
\bigjoin_{\substack{F\in S\\G\in T}}
[\Xi(F)\fatsemi\Xi(G)]
\\
&=
\bigjoin_{\substack{F\in S\\G\in T}}
[\Xi(F\fatsemi G)],
\end{aligned}
\]
where the last equality follows from functoriality of \(\Xi\). Put
\[
S\fatsemi T
\defeq
\left\{
F\fatsemi G
\;\middle|\;
F\in S,\ G\in T
\right\}.
\]
Associativity, commutativity and idempotence of \(\join\) now give
\[
\bigjoin_{\substack{F\in S\\G\in T}}
[\Xi(F\fatsemi G)]
=
\bigjoin_{H\in S\fatsemi T}[\Xi(H)]
=
[\operatorname{Can}(S\fatsemi T)].
\]
Consequently,
\[
\Psi([\mathcal F]\fatsemi[\mathcal G])
=
S\fatsemi T
=
\Psi([\mathcal F])\fatsemi\Psi([\mathcal G]).
\]

Similarly, bilinearity of tensor and strict monoidality of \(\Xi\)
give
\[
\begin{aligned}
[\mathcal F]\otimes[\mathcal G]
&=
\bigjoin_{\substack{F\in S\\G\in T}}
[\Xi(F)\otimes\Xi(G)]
\\
&=
\bigjoin_{\substack{F\in S\\G\in T}}
[\Xi(F\otimes G)]
\\
&=
[\operatorname{Can}(S\otimes T)],
\end{aligned}
\]
where
\[
S\otimes T
\defeq
\left\{
F\otimes G
\;\middle|\;
F\in S,\ G\in T
\right\}.
\]
Hence
\[
\Psi([\mathcal F]\otimes[\mathcal G])
=
\Psi([\mathcal F])\otimes\Psi([\mathcal G]).
\]

Moreover,
\[
\Psi([\id_n])
=
\Psi([\Xi(\id_n)])
=
\{\id_n\}.
\]
Since \(\Xi\) preserves the symmetries,
\[
\Psi([\sym_{n,m}])
=
\{\sym_{n,m}\}.
\]
The functor \(\Psi\) is identity-on-objects, so it also preserves the
monoidal unit \(0\). It follows that \(\Psi\) is an identity-on-objects
strict symmetric monoidal \(\catname{SLat}\)-functor.

It remains to prove that \(\Phi\) and \(\Psi\) are inverse. For every
finite non-empty subset \(S\),
\[
\Psi(\Phi(S))
=
\Psi([\operatorname{Can}(S)])
=
S.
\]
Conversely, let \([\mathcal G]\) be a morphism of the quotient and put
\[
S\defeq\Psi([\mathcal G]).
\]
By the definition of \(\Psi\),
\[
[\mathcal G]=[\operatorname{Can}(S)].
\]
Therefore
\[
\Phi(\Psi([\mathcal G]))
=
\Phi(S)
=
[\operatorname{Can}(S)]
=
[\mathcal G].
\]
Thus \(\Phi\) and \(\Psi\) are mutually inverse isomorphisms of
\(\catname{SLat}\)-PROPs.

Consequently, the composite
\[
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}(\llangle-\rrangle)
\fatsemi
\Phi
:
\mathbf H_{\Sigma}^{\join}
\longrightarrow
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R
\]
is the required isomorphism. Its inverse is
\[
\Psi
\fatsemi
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}
(\llangle-\rrangle^{-1}).
\]
\end{proof}

Thus, extended cospans modulo the structural rewrites
$\mathfrak R$ provide a sound and complete combinatorial presentation of
the free Frobenius $\catname{SLat}$-PROP
$\mathbf H_{\Sigma}^{\join}$.
In particular, every morphism of $\mathbf H_{\Sigma}^{\join}$ is
represented by an extended cospan, and two such representations determine
the same morphism precisely when they are identified in the quotient by
$\mathfrak R$.
This provides a combinatorial semantics for string diagrams in the free
$\catname{SLat}$-enriched symmetric monoidal category with Frobenius
structure generated by $\Sigma$.
We next define a combinatorial semantics for rewriting such string diagrams
and show that it captures $\Sigma$-term rewriting modulo the Frobenius,
symmetric monoidal and $\catname{SLat}$ axioms.

\section{The rewriting correspondence for Frobenius theories}

Write
\[
I
\defeq
\widetilde F_{\catname{SLat}}(\llangle-\rrangle)
\fatsemi
\Phi
:
\mathbf H_{\Sigma}^{\join}
\longrightarrow
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)/\mathfrak R
\]
for the isomorphism of
\cref{theorem:ehypi_slat_isomorphism}.

\subsection{Rewriting modulo structural rules}
\begin{definition}[EDPOI rewriting modulo structural rules]
\label{def:edpoi_modulo_structural}
Let
\(\mathcal A,\mathcal B:n\to m\) and
\(\mathcal L,\mathcal R:i\to j\)
be extended cospans. We write
\[
\mathcal A
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
  \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle
}
\mathcal B
\]
if there exist extended cospans
\(\mathcal A',\mathcal B',\mathcal L',\mathcal R'\) such that
\[
\mathcal A\sim_{\mathfrak R}\mathcal A',
\qquad
\mathcal B'\sim_{\mathfrak R}\mathcal B,
\qquad
\mathcal L\sim_{\mathfrak R}\mathcal L',
\qquad
\mathcal R\sim_{\mathfrak R}\mathcal R',
\]
and
\[
\mathcal A'
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \langle\mathcal L',\mathcal R'\rangle
}
\mathcal B'
\]
is an EDPOI rewrite.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}
Whenever an \(\catname{SLat}\)-PROP or an
\(\catname{SLat}\)-functor is compared with an ordinary category or
functor, we implicitly regard it through its underlying ordinary
category.
Since the action of the underlying functor of an $\catname{SLat}$-functor is given by the action of the latter, the underlying functor in particular preserves joins and is an isomorphism when the original functor is.
\end{remark}

The functor
\[
\llangle-\rrangle:
\mathbf H_\Sigma\longrightarrow\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)
\]
can be applied directly to morphisms of \(\mathbf H_\Sigma\).
Indeed, the symmetric monoidal and Frobenius equations by which its
terms are identified are already satisfied by cospans of hypergraphs, and
the interpretation produces a concrete cospan, up to
isomorphism.

The situation is different in the enriched setting. 
The isomorphism $I$ assigns to a morphism of \(\mathbf H^{\join}_\Sigma\) an equivalence class
of extended cospans, rather than a particular extended cospan.
However, EDPOI rewriting is performed on concrete representatives
before passing to the quotient.

Furthermore, the structural interpretation of a term does not
descend directly through the \(\catname{SLat}\)-equations. 
For example, terms related by associativity or idempotence of \(\join\) may determine
non-isomorphic extended cospans with different box structure; these
cospans become equal only after quotienting by the structural rewrites
\(\mathfrak R\). 
We therefore define the concrete interpretation on raw
terms, retaining their chosen syntactic structure, and subsequently show
that its image in the quotient depends only on the represented morphism. 
With this in mind,~\cref{def:edpoi_modulo_structural} may be seen as a separation of two forms of structural equality present in the syntax.
Symmetric monoidal and Frobenius equations are absorbed directly by the interpretation of terms as cospans, whereas the semilattice equations (except for commutativity one) are accounted for by allowing structural rewrites before and after the EDPOI step, as well as on the rules.

\subsection{Concrete representatives of raw terms}

\begin{definition}[Combinatorial interpretation of raw terms]
For \(m,n\in\mathbb N\), let
\(\operatorname{Tm}^{\join}_{\Sigma}(m,n)\) denote the set of raw
well-typed \(\Sigma^{\join}\)-terms \(m\to n\). For
\(t\in\operatorname{Tm}^{\join}_{\Sigma}(m,n)\), write
\[
[t]\in\mathbf H^{\join}_\Sigma(m,n)
\]
for the morphism represented by \(t\).
We will define a concrete interpretation of raw terms as extended cospans, given by a function
\[
\mathrm{Rep}_{m,n} : \operatorname{Tm}^{\join}_{\Sigma}(m,n) \longrightarrow \catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)(m,n)~,
\]
we will omit writing the subscripts moving forward.

Let
\[
\Sigma^{+}
\defeq
\Sigma\coprod\Sigma_{\mathrm{Frob}}
\]
be the signature containing the ordinary generators and the Frobenius
generators. For every generator \(g:m\to n\) of \(\Sigma^{+}\), write
\[
\overline g:m\to n
\]
for its canonical image in \(\mathbf H_\Sigma\), and define
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(g)
\defeq
\Xi\bigl(\llangle\overline g\rrangle\bigr).
\]
The identities and symmetries are sent to the corresponding identities
and symmetries in \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\), and define
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{Rep}(s\fatsemi t)
  &\defeq\mathrm{Rep}(s)\fatsemi\mathrm{Rep}(t),\\
\mathrm{Rep}(s\otimes t)
  &\defeq\mathrm{Rep}(s)\otimes\mathrm{Rep}(t),\\
\mathrm{Rep}(s\join t)
  &\defeq\mathrm{Rep}(s)\join\mathrm{Rep}(t).
\end{aligned}
\]

For a join-free raw term \(t\), let
\[
[t]_0\in\mathbf H_\Sigma
\]
denote its equivalence class modulo the symmetric monoidal and
Frobenius equations. Then
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(t)
=
\Xi\bigl(\llangle[t]_0\rrangle\bigr).
\]
Thus the restriction of $\mathrm{Rep}$ to join-free raw terms factors
through the functor
\[
\mathbf H_\Sigma
\xrightarrow{\llangle-\rrangle}
\catname{HypI}(\Sigma)
\xrightarrow{\Xi}
\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Compatibility with denotation]
\label{lemma:concrete_interpretation_quotient}
For every raw \(\Sigma^{\join}\)-term \(t\),
\[
Q(\mathrm{Rep}(t))=I([t]).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a generator $g$ of $\Sigma^{+}$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
I([g])
&=
\Phi\bigl(\{\llangle \overline{g}\rrangle\}\bigr)\\
&=
Q\bigl(\Xi(\llangle \overline{g}\rrangle)\bigr)\\
&=
Q(\mathrm{Rep}(g)).
\end{aligned}
\]
The remaining cases follow by induction on the construction of \(t\).
The interpretation \(\mathrm{Rep}\) is compositional, \(I\) preserves
composition, tensor and join, and the quotient map satisfies
\[
\begin{aligned}
Q(\mathcal F\fatsemi\mathcal G)
 &=Q(\mathcal F)\fatsemi Q(\mathcal G),\\
Q(\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal G)
 &=Q(\mathcal F)\otimes Q(\mathcal G),\\
Q(\mathcal F\join\mathcal G)
 &=Q(\mathcal F)\join Q(\mathcal G).
\end{aligned}
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Invariance under the underlying PROP equations]
\label{lemma:concrete_interpretation_smc}
Suppose raw \(\Sigma^{\join}\)-terms \(s,t:m\to n\) are equal using only
the symmetric monoidal and Frobenius equations, including instances in
contexts containing joins. Then
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(s)=\mathrm{Rep}(t)
\]
in \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We proceed by induction on the equational derivation. Instances of the
symmetric monoidal equations are preserved because
\(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\) is a PROP. 
% This remains true when
% the morphism variables in such an equation are instantiated by terms
% containing joins, since their interpretations are still morphisms of
% that PROP.

The generating Frobenius equations are join-free. On their two sides,
\(\mathrm{Rep}\) agrees with
\(\llangle-\rrangle\fatsemi\Xi\), so the equations hold because
\(\llangle-\rrangle\) interprets the Frobenius generators as the
canonical Frobenius cospans and \(\Xi\) is strict symmetric monoidal.

Finally, equality is preserved by composition and tensor, and join is
well defined on morphisms of
\(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\). Hence the equality remains valid
when a generating equation is placed in any composition, tensor or
join context.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Realisation of term equations]
\label{lemma:term_equations_realised}
If \(s,t:m\to n\) are raw \(\Sigma^{\join}\)-terms satisfying
\[
[s]=[t]
\]
in \(\mathbf H^{\join}_\Sigma\), then
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(s)\sim_{\mathfrak R}\mathrm{Rep}(t).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \cref{lemma:concrete_interpretation_quotient},
\[
Q(\mathrm{Rep}(s))
=
I([s])
=
I([t])
=
Q(\mathrm{Rep}(t)).
\]
By the definition of \(Q\), this equality holds precisely when
\(\mathrm{Rep}(s)\) and \(\mathrm{Rep}(t)\) belong to the same
\(\sim_{\mathfrak R}\)-class.

Concretely, the symmetric monoidal and Frobenius equations are already
preserved by \cref{lemma:concrete_interpretation_smc}, whereas the
equations involving the semilattice structure are realised by the
structural rules generating \(\mathfrak R\).
\end{proof}

The preceding lemmas provide the required passage between syntax and
concrete representatives. The function $\mathrm{Rep}$ remembers the
chosen syntax of a raw term, while the quotient functor $Q$ removes
this choice: symmetric monoidal and Frobenius equations already hold
between concrete representatives, and the remaining semilattice
equations are realised by $\mathfrak R$.

To compare syntactic rewriting with EDPOI rewriting, it remains to
relate the usual decomposition of a string diagram around a redex to a
corresponding decomposition of its e-hypergraph interpretation. As in
the join-free setting, this is most conveniently done after bending the
external input interface.

\subsection{Bending and compatibility with contexts}

Just as in~\cref{theorem:dpoi_rewriting_frob}, we will consider the interpretation of terms with bent inputs.
That is, given a raw $\Sigma^{\join}$-term $t : m \to n$ we will let
\[
\corner{t} \defeq \cup_m \fatsemi (\id_m \otimes t) : 0 \longrightarrow m+n~.
\]
The extended cospan counterpart of this bending requires some care to account for internal interfaces.
That is, given an extended cospan
\[
n \xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{ext}}} n' \xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{int}}} \mathcal{F} \xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{int}}} m' \xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{ext}}} m
\]
we will fold the external interfaces as
\[
0 \xrightarrow{} n' \setminus n \xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{sint}}} \mathcal{F} \xleftarrow{[f, g_{\mathrm{int}}]} n \coprod m' \xleftarrow{\id_n \coprod g_{\mathrm{ext}}} n \coprod m~,
\]
where $f_{\mathrm{sint}}$ is the restriction of $f_{\mathrm{int}}$ to the strictly internal part of the input interface, and $f = f_{\mathrm{ext}} \fatsemi f_{\mathrm{int}}$.
This is isomorphic to a cospan given by
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(\cup_n) \fatsemi (\id_n \otimes \mathcal{F})~.
\]
Given an extended cospan $\mathcal{F} : n \longrightarrow m$, we will write $\corner{\mathcal{F}} : 0 \longrightarrow{} n \coprod m$ for its folding.


\begin{lemma}[Compatibility with bending]
\label{lemma:concrete_interpretation_bending}
For every raw $\Sigma^{\join}$-term \(t:m\to n\), one has
\[
[\corner t]=\corner{[t]}.
\]
Moreover, for parallel morphisms
\(f,g:m\to n\) of \(\mathbf H^{\join}_\Sigma\), bending preserves joins:
\[
\corner{(f\join g)}
=
\corner f\join\corner g.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For the first equality,
\[
\begin{aligned}
[\corner t]
&=
\bigl[
\cup_m\fatsemi(\id_m\otimes t)
\bigr]\\
&=
[\cup_m]\fatsemi
\bigl(\id_m\otimes[t]\bigr)\\
&=
\cup_m\fatsemi
\bigl(\id_m\otimes[t]\bigr)\\
&=
\corner{[t]},
\end{aligned}
\]
because term denotation preserves composition and tensor and sends the
raw Frobenius cup to the corresponding cup in
\(\mathbf H^{\join}_\Sigma\).

For the second equality,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\corner{(f\join g)}
&=
\cup_m\fatsemi
\bigl(\id_m\otimes(f\join g)\bigr)\\
&=
\cup_m\fatsemi
\bigl(
(\id_m\otimes f)\join(\id_m\otimes g)
\bigr)\\
&=
\bigl(\cup_m\fatsemi(\id_m\otimes f)\bigr)
\join
\bigl(\cup_m\fatsemi(\id_m\otimes g)\bigr)\\
&=
\corner f\join\corner g,
\end{aligned}
\]
by bilinearity of tensor and composition.
\end{proof}

We begin with the elementary EDPOI step corresponding to an application
of a rule to its own left-hand side. Subsequent occurrences of the rule
will be obtained from this step by placing it in the appropriate
composition, tensor and join contexts.

\begin{lemma}[Identity application]
\label{lemma:edpoi_identity_application}
Let \(l,r:i\to j\) be raw \(\Sigma^{\join}\)-terms. There is an EDPOI
rewrite
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner l)
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \left\langle
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner l),
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)
  \right\rangle
}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner r).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
First observe that if \(\mathrm{Rep}(l)\) produces an extended cospan
\[
i
\xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{ext}}}
i'
\xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{int}}}
\mathcal L
\xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{int}}}
j'
\xleftarrow{g_{\mathrm{ext}}}
j,
\]
then \(\mathrm{Rep}(\corner l)\) produces an extended cospan isomorphic to
\[
0
\longrightarrow
i'\setminus i
\xrightarrow{f_{\mathrm{sint}}}
\mathcal L
\xleftarrow{[f,g_{\mathrm{int}}]}
i\coprod j'
\xleftarrow{\id_i\coprod g_{\mathrm{ext}}}
i\coprod j.
\]

Say the source of the rule is given by the cospan
\[
0
\xrightarrow{!_{i'\setminus i}}
i'\setminus i
\xrightarrow{f_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{sint}}}
\mathcal L
\xleftarrow{[f_{\mathcal L},g_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{int}}]}
i\coprod j'
\xleftarrow{\id_i\coprod g_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{ext}}}
i\coprod j,
\]
and the target is given by
\[
0
\xrightarrow{!_{i''\setminus i}}
i''\setminus i
\xrightarrow{f_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{sint}}}
\mathcal R
\xleftarrow{[f_{\mathcal R},g_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}]}
i\coprod j''
\xleftarrow{\id_i\coprod g_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}}}
i\coprod j.
\]

Let
\[
J\defeq i\coprod j,
\qquad
I_1\defeq i'\setminus i,
\qquad
J_1\defeq i\coprod j',
\qquad
I_2\defeq i''\setminus i,
\qquad
J_2\defeq i\coprod j''.
\]
Write
\[
q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{int}}
\defeq
[f_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{sint}},
 f_{\mathcal L},
 g_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{int}}]
:
I_1\coprod J_1\longrightarrow\mathcal L
\]
and
\[
q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}
\defeq
[f_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{sint}},
 f_{\mathcal R},
 g_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}]
:
I_2\coprod J_2\longrightarrow\mathcal R.
\]
If \(\iota_{J_1}\) and \(\iota_{J_2}\) denote the corresponding
coprojections, put
\[
q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{ext}}
\defeq
(\id_i\coprod g_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{ext}})
\fatsemi\iota_{J_1}
:
J\longrightarrow I_1\coprod J_1
\]
and
\[
q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}}
\defeq
(\id_i\coprod g_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}})
\fatsemi\iota_{J_2}
:
J\longrightarrow I_2\coprod J_2.
\]
Together they assemble into the top side of an EDPOI diagram as
\[
\mathcal L
\xleftarrow{q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{int}}}
I_1\coprod J_1
\xleftarrow{q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{ext}}}
J
\xrightarrow{q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}}}
I_2\coprod J_2
\xrightarrow{q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}}
\mathcal R.
\]

Take
\[
m\defeq\id_{\mathcal L}
\]
with the induced anchor \(\top_{\mathcal L}\), and put
\[
\lambda_{\mathcal L}
\defeq
q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{ext}}
\fatsemi q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{int}},
\qquad
\lambda_{\mathcal R}
\defeq
q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}}
\fatsemi q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}.
\]
We then get the following EDPOI diagram:
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
{\mathcal L}
&
{I_1\coprod J_1}
&
J
&
{I_2\coprod J_2}
&
{\mathcal R}
\\
\\
{\mathcal L}
&&
J
&&
{\mathcal R}
\\
&
{I_1\coprod J_1}
&
J
&
{I_2\coprod J_2}
\\
&&
J
\arrow["m"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{int}}}"', from=1-2, to=1-1]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{ext}}}"', from=1-3, to=1-2]
\arrow["{\id_J}", from=1-3, to=3-3]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}}}", from=1-3, to=1-4]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}}", from=1-4, to=1-5]
\arrow["{\id_{\mathcal R}}", from=1-5, to=3-5]
\arrow["{\lambda_{\mathcal L}}"', from=3-3, to=3-1]
\arrow["{\lambda_{\mathcal R}}", from=3-3, to=3-5]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{int}}}", from=4-2, to=3-1]
\arrow["{\id_J}", from=4-3, to=3-3]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}}"', from=4-4, to=3-5]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal L,\mathrm{ext}}}", from=5-3, to=4-2]
\arrow["{\id_J}", from=5-3, to=4-3]
\arrow["{q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}}}"', from=5-3, to=4-4]
\arrow[
  "\lrcorner"{anchor=center,pos=0.125,rotate=90},
  draw=none,
  from=3-1,
  to=1-3
]
\arrow[
  "\lrcorner"{anchor=center,pos=0.125,rotate=180},
  draw=none,
  from=3-5,
  to=1-3
]
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where the two squares are trivial pushouts.

The arrows
\[
J
\xrightarrow{\id_J}
J
\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathcal L}}
\mathcal L
\]
then form an extended pushout complement. Condition~(1) is satisfied
because the left square is a pushout and \(\id_J\) is anchored.
Conditions~(2) and~(3) are trivially satisfied because the match is
the identity.

For condition~(4), we have
\[
S_{\mathrm{in}}\cong I_1,
\qquad
S_{\mathrm{out}}\cong J_1\setminus J,
\]
so that
\[
I_1\setminus S_{\mathrm{in}}\cong0,
\qquad
J_1\setminus S_{\mathrm{out}}\cong J.
\]
Thus the retained interfaces equip the complement \(J\) with the
identity extended cospan from \(0\) to \(J\), which gives the required
factorisation on the nose.

For condition~(5), take
\[
\alpha_{\mathrm{in}}
\defeq
\id_{I_1}
:
I_1\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{in}}
\]
and
\[
\alpha_{\mathrm{out}}
\defeq
\id_{J_1\setminus J}
:
J_1\setminus J\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{out}},
\]
under the displayed identifications. The required diagrams commute
because \(m=\id_{\mathcal L}\), and the interface orders are unchanged.

According to the EDPOI definition, the new internal input and output
interfaces are
\[
I_2\coprod0\cong I_2
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
J\coprod(J_2\setminus J)\cong J_2,
\]
respectively. The second pushout therefore gives the extended cospan
\(\mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)\), with external and internal legs
\(q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{ext}}\) and
\(q_{\mathcal R,\mathrm{int}}\). Thus
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner l)
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \left\langle
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner l),
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)
  \right\rangle
}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner r),
\]
as required.
\end{proof}

As mentioned, a single-step rewrite described above can be placed in any context modulo the structural rules.
\begin{lemma}[Congruence modulo structural rules]
\label{lemma:rewriting_modulo_congruence}
Suppose
\[
\mathcal F
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
 \langle\mathcal L, \mathcal R\rangle}
\mathcal G.
\]
Then, whenever the displayed operations are defined,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal C\fatsemi\mathcal F
&\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
 \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle}
\mathcal C\fatsemi\mathcal G,\\
\mathcal F\fatsemi\mathcal C
&\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
 \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle}
\mathcal G\fatsemi\mathcal C,\\
\mathcal C\otimes\mathcal F
&\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
 \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle}
\mathcal C\otimes\mathcal G,\\
\mathcal F\otimes\mathcal C
&\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
 \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle}
\mathcal G\otimes\mathcal C,
\end{aligned}
\]
and
\[
\mathcal F^1\join\cdots\join\mathcal F\join\cdots\join\mathcal F^p
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
 \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle}
\mathcal F^1\join\cdots\join\mathcal G\join\cdots\join\mathcal F^p.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose witnesses
\(\mathcal F',\mathcal G',\mathcal L',\mathcal R'\) from
\cref{def:edpoi_modulo_structural}. Since
\(\sim_{\mathfrak R}\) is a congruence, the corresponding composites,
tensors and joins are structurally equivalent to those formed with
\(\mathcal F'\) and \(\mathcal G'\). The required direct EDPOI rewrite
between the latter is supplied by
\cref{lemma:rewriting_congruence}. These data are precisely the
witnesses required by \cref{def:edpoi_modulo_structural}.
\end{proof}

% Just as in~\cref{theorem:dpoi_rewriting_frob}, we will consider the interpretation of terms with bent inputs.
% The extended cospan counterpart of this bending requires some care to account for internal interfaces.
% That is, given an extended cospan
% \[
% n \xrightarrow{} n' \xrightarrow{} \mathcal{F} \xleftarrow{} m' \xleftarrow{} m
% \]
% we will fold the external interfaces as
% \[
% 0 \xrightarrow{} n' \setminus n \xrightarrow{} \mathcal{F} \xleftarrow{} n \coprod m' \xleftarrow{} n \coprod m~.
% \]
% This is isomorphic to a cospan given by
% \[
% \mathrm{Rep}(\cup_n) \fatsemi (\id_n \otimes \mathcal{F})~.
% \]
% Given an extended cospan $\mathcal{F} : n \longrightarrow m$, we will write $\corner{\mathcal{F}} : 0 \longrightarrow{} n \coprod m$ for its folding.


% For an extended cospan \(\mathbf G:u\to v\), write
% \[
% \corner{\mathbf G}
% \defeq
% \cup_u
% \fatsemi
% (\id_u\otimes\mathbf G)
% :
% 0\longrightarrow u+v
% \]
% for its categorical folding. This folds only the external input
% interface; composition in
% \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\) leaves all strictly internal
% interfaces on their respective sides.

% \begin{lemma}[Compatibility of EDPOI rewriting with bending]
% \label{lemma:edpoi_bending}
% If
% \[
% \mathbf G
% \;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
%   \langle\mathbf L,\mathbf R\rangle
% }
% \mathbf H
% \]
% is an EDPOI rewrite between extended cospans \(u\to v\), then
% \[
% \corner{\mathbf G}
% \;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
%   \langle\mathbf L,\mathbf R\rangle
% }
% \corner{\mathbf H}.
% \]
% The same statement holds for rewriting modulo \(\mathfrak R\).
% \end{lemma}

% \begin{proof}
% By~\cref{lemma:rewriting_congruence}, tensoring the rewrite with
% \(\id_u\) gives
% \[
% \id_u\otimes\mathbf G
% \;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
%   \langle\mathbf L,\mathbf R\rangle
% }
% \id_u\otimes\mathbf H.
% \]
% Precomposing with \(\cup_u\) and applying the composition clause of the
% same lemma gives the required rewrite. The statement modulo
% \(\mathfrak R\) follows from
% \cref{lemma:rewriting_modulo_congruence}.
% \end{proof}
The above results suffice for the soundness direction. 
For completeness, we must recover a syntactic redex decomposition from an arbitrary
EDPOI match. 
We distinguish whether the match occurs at the root of the
carrier or inside one or more $\boxsort$-edges. 
The following lemma handles the root case.

\subsection{Factorisation of matches}

\begin{lemma}[Root factorisation]
\label{lemma:root_edpoi_factorisation}
Suppose
\[
\mathcal F
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle
}
\mathcal G
\]
is an EDPOI rewrite, where
\[
\mathcal F,\mathcal G:0\to n,
\qquad
\mathcal L,\mathcal R:0\to j,
\]
and suppose that the match anchor is the root of the carrier of
\(\mathcal F\). Then there is a well-typed extended cospan
\[
\mathcal C:j\longrightarrow n
\]
such that
\[
\mathcal F=\mathcal L\fatsemi\mathcal C,
\qquad
\mathcal G=\mathcal R\fatsemi\mathcal C
\]
in \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\). Consequently,
\[
Q(\mathcal F)
=
Q(\mathcal L)\fatsemi Q(\mathcal C),
\qquad
Q(\mathcal G)
=
Q(\mathcal R)\fatsemi Q(\mathcal C).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write the rule cospans as
\begin{gather*}
0
\longrightarrow
I_{\mathcal L}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal L
\longleftarrow
O_{\mathcal L}
\longleftarrow
j\\
0
\longrightarrow
I_{\mathcal R}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal R
\longleftarrow
O_{\mathcal R}
\longleftarrow
j
\end{gather*}
and the ambient cospans as
\begin{gather*}
0
\longrightarrow
I_{\mathcal F}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal F
\longleftarrow
O_{\mathcal F}
\longleftarrow
n\\
0
\longrightarrow
I_{\mathcal G}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal G
\longleftarrow
O_{\mathcal G}
\longleftarrow
n~.
\end{gather*}
Let
\[
m:\mathcal L\longrightarrow\mathcal F
\]
be the match; it is strict since the anchor is $\top_{\mathcal{F}}$ by assumption. 
Let \(S_I\) and \(S_O\) be the affected input and output
internal interfaces supplied by the extended pushout complement, and
put
\[
\overline I
\defeq
I_{\mathcal F}\setminus S_I,
\qquad
\overline O
\defeq
O_{\mathcal F}\setminus S_O.
\]
Write
\[
g_I^\bot:
\overline I\longrightarrow\mathcal L^\bot,
\qquad
g_O^\bot:
\overline O\longrightarrow\mathcal L^\bot
\]
for the retained interface legs, and write
\[
\overline g_{\mathrm{ext}}^O:
n\longrightarrow\overline O
\]
for the corestriction of the ambient output external leg.

Let
\[
c:j\longrightarrow\mathcal L^\bot
\]
be the anchored leg of the extended pushout complement; it is strict because $m$ is.
Define
\[
\mathcal C
\defeq
\left(
j
\xrightarrow{\iota_1}
j\coprod\overline I
\xrightarrow{[c,g_I^\bot]}
\mathcal L^\bot
\xleftarrow{g_O^\bot}
\overline O
\xleftarrow{\overline g_{\mathrm{ext}}^O}
n
\right).
\]
This is a well-typed extended cospan because the extended pushout complement yields a well-typed extended cospan and the image of $j$ is top-level, since $c$ is strict, so it does not affect well-typedness.
The data above assemble into the following diagram
% https://q.uiver.app/#q=WzAsMTIsWzIsMCwiaiJdLFsxLDAsIklfe1xcbWF0aGNhbHtMfX0gXFxjb3Byb2QgT197XFxtYXRoY2Fse0x9fSJdLFswLDAsIlxcbWF0aGNhbHtMfSJdLFszLDAsIklfe1xcbWF0aGNhbHtSfX0gXFxjb3Byb2QgT197XFxtYXRoY2Fse1J9fSJdLFs0LDAsIlxcbWF0aGNhbHtSfSJdLFswLDIsIlxcbWF0aGNhbHtGfSJdLFsyLDIsIlxcbWF0aGNhbHtMfV57XFxib3R9Il0sWzQsMiwiXFxtYXRoY2Fse0d9Il0sWzIsNCwibiJdLFsxLDMsIklfe1xcbWF0aGNhbHtGfX1cXGNvcHJvZCBPX3tcXG1hdGhjYWx7Rn19Il0sWzMsMywiSV97XFxtYXRoY2Fse0d9fVxcY29wcm9kIE9fe1xcbWF0aGNhbHtHfX0iXSxbMiwzLCJcXG92ZXJsaW5le0l9XFxjb3Byb2QgXFxvdmVybGluZXtPfSJdLFswLDFdLFsxLDJdLFszLDRdLFswLDNdLFsyLDUsIm0iLDJdLFs2LDVdLFswLDYsImMiXSxbNCw3XSxbNiw3XSxbNSwwLCIiLDAseyJzdHlsZSI6eyJuYW1lIjoiY29ybmVyIn19XSxbNywwLCIiLDIseyJzdHlsZSI6eyJuYW1lIjoiY29ybmVyIn19XSxbOCw5XSxbOSw1XSxbMTAsN10sWzgsMTBdLFs4LDExLCJcXGJhcntnfV57T31fe1xcbWF0aHJte2V4dH19IiwyXSxbMTEsNiwiZ197SX1ee1xcYm90fSBcXGNvcHJvZCBnX3tPfV57XFxib3R9IiwyXV0=
\[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\mathcal{L}} & {I_{\mathcal{L}} \coprod O_{\mathcal{L}}} & j & {I_{\mathcal{R}} \coprod O_{\mathcal{R}}} & {\mathcal{R}} \\
	\\
	{\mathcal{F}} && {\mathcal{L}^{\bot}} && {\mathcal{G}} \\
	& {I_{\mathcal{F}}\coprod O_{\mathcal{F}}} & {\overline{I}\coprod \overline{O}} & {I_{\mathcal{G}}\coprod O_{\mathcal{G}}} \\
	&& n
	\arrow["m"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=1-2, to=1-1]
	\arrow[from=1-3, to=1-2]
	\arrow[from=1-3, to=1-4]
	\arrow["c", from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow[from=1-4, to=1-5]
	\arrow[from=1-5, to=3-5]
	\arrow["\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=90}, draw=none, from=3-1, to=1-3]
	\arrow[from=3-3, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=3-3, to=3-5]
	\arrow["\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=180}, draw=none, from=3-5, to=1-3]
	\arrow[from=4-2, to=3-1]
	\arrow["{g_{I}^{\bot} \coprod g_{O}^{\bot}}"', from=4-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow[from=4-4, to=3-5]
	\arrow[from=5-3, to=4-2]
	\arrow["{\bar{g}^{O}_{\mathrm{ext}}}"', from=5-3, to=4-3]
	\arrow[from=5-3, to=4-4]
\end{tikzcd}~.
\]

Since the cospans $\mathcal{L}$ and $\mathcal{C}$ share the interface, we can form their composite in $\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)$
\[
\mathcal L\fatsemi\mathcal C~.
\]
The carrier of this composite is exactly $\mathcal{F}$ because of the left square of the diagram above.
Its input and output internal interfaces are, up to canonical reassociation,
\[
I_{\mathcal L}\coprod\overline I
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\overline O\coprod(O_{\mathcal L}\setminus j).
\]

Condition~(5) supplies blockwise order-preserving isomorphisms
\[
\alpha_I:
I_{\mathcal L}\xrightarrow{\cong}S_I,
\qquad
\alpha_O:
O_{\mathcal L}\setminus j\xrightarrow{\cong}S_O.
\]
They induce isomorphisms
\[
\alpha_I\coprod\id_{\overline I}:
I_{\mathcal L}\coprod\overline I
\xrightarrow{\cong}
S_I\coprod\overline I
\cong I_{\mathcal F}
\]
and
\[
\id_{\overline O}\coprod\alpha_O:
\overline O\coprod(O_{\mathcal L}\setminus j)
\xrightarrow{\cong}
\overline O\coprod S_O
\cong O_{\mathcal F}.
\]
The diagrams in condition~(5) show that these isomorphisms commute with
the internal legs, while condition~(4) gives commutativity on the
retained interfaces. They preserve the order within every block, and
the output-interface isomorphism is the identity on the image of the
external interface \(n\). Together with the identity on the carrier
\(\mathcal F\), they therefore define an isomorphism of extended
cospans
\[
\mathcal L\fatsemi\mathcal C\cong\mathcal F.
\]
Since morphisms of \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\) are
isomorphism classes of extended cospans,
\[
\mathcal F=\mathcal L\fatsemi\mathcal C.
\]
Since \(Q\) preserves composition,
\[
Q(\mathcal F)
=
Q(\mathcal L)\fatsemi Q(\mathcal C).
\]

For the right-hand side, the second EDPOI pushout is exactly the carrier
pushout computing
\[
\mathcal R\fatsemi\mathcal C.
\]
Moreover, the definition of the EDPOI result equips this pushout with
the retained interfaces \(\overline I,\overline O\), together with the
strictly internal interfaces of \(\mathcal R\). These are precisely the
interfaces produced by the displayed composite. Hence
\[
\mathcal R\fatsemi\mathcal C
\cong
\mathcal G~.
\]
Consequently,
\[
Q(\mathcal G)
=
Q(\mathcal R)\fatsemi Q(\mathcal C).
\]
\end{proof}

When the match is nested, root factorisation cannot yet be applied to
the whole carrier. 
We instead isolate the outermost box enclosing the
match. 
Its unchanged components form a common join context, while the
component containing the match carries a rewrite with one fewer
enclosing box. 
This is the content of the following decomposition
lemma.

\begin{lemma}[Decomposition at an outermost enclosing box]
\label{lemma:outermost_box_decomposition}
Suppose
\[
\mathcal F
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle
}
\mathcal G
\]
is an EDPOI rewrite, where
\[
\mathcal F,\mathcal G:0\to n,
\qquad
\mathcal L,\mathcal R:0\to j,
\]
and suppose that the match anchor is not the root of the carrier of
\(\mathcal F\).

Let \(p\) be the outermost \(\boxsort\)-edge whose contents contain the
image of the match, and put
\[
u\defeq |s(p)|,
\qquad
v\defeq |t(p)|.
\]
Let
\[
e\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal F}(p)
\]
be the unique component containing the image of the match. 
The anchor need not be $e$ itself; the match may lie further down the
hierarchy below $e$.
Then there are well-typed component cospans
\[
\mathcal F_d:u\to v
\qquad
\bigl(d\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal F}(p)\bigr),
\]
a well-typed extended cospan
\[
\mathcal G_e:u\to v,
\]
and a well-typed context cospan
\[
\mathcal D:u \coprod v\to n
\]
such that:
\begin{enumerate}
\item there is an EDPOI rewrite
\[
\mathcal F_e
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle
}
\mathcal G_e
\]
whose match has fewer enclosing \(\boxsort\)-edges;

\item every component \(d\neq e\) is unchanged;

\item writing
\[
\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}
\defeq
\bigjoin_{d\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal F}(p)}
\mathcal F_d
\]
and
\[
\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}
\defeq
\mathcal G_e
\join
\bigjoin_{\substack{
  d\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal F}(p)\\
  d\neq e
}}
\mathcal F_d,
\]
one has
\[
\mathcal F
=
\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}}
\fatsemi\mathcal D
\]
and
\[
\mathcal G
=
\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}}
\fatsemi\mathcal D
\]
in \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\). Consequently,
\[
Q(\mathcal F)
=
Q(\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}})
\fatsemi Q(\mathcal D)
\]
and
\[
Q(\mathcal G)
=
Q(\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}})
\fatsemi Q(\mathcal D).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For each
\[
d\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal F}(p),
\]
let \(\mathcal F_d:u\to v\) be the component cospan determined by \(d\),
as in~\cref{def:flat_resolutions}, and form
\[
\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}
\defeq
\bigjoin_{d\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal F}(p)}
\mathcal F_d.
\]

The extended cospan \(\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}\) has a distinguished
\(\boxsort\)-edge with formal ordered boundary \(u\to v\). Write
\(P_{\mathcal F}\) for the carrier e-hypergraph of
\(\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}}\). There is a canonical homomorphism
\[
\chi_p:
P_{\mathcal F}\longrightarrow\mathcal F
\]
sending the distinguished \(\boxsort\)-edge to \(p\), its components
to the corresponding components of \(p\), and its formal boundary
ports to the entries of \(s(p)\) and \(t(p)\).

% The map \(\chi_p\) need not be injective on this formal boundary. For
% example, if a source and a target of \(p\) are the same vertex of
% \(\mathcal F\), the corresponding formal ports have the same image.

Consider the rewrite rule given by $\langle \corner{\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{F}}},  \corner{\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{F}}}\rangle$, matched into $\mathcal{F}$ by $\chi_p$.
This induces an EDPOI diagram as
% https://q.uiver.app/#q=WzAsNyxbMCwwLCJcXGNvcm5lcntcXG1hdGhjYWwgUF97XFxtYXRoY2FsIEZ9fSJdLFswLDIsIlxcbWF0aGNhbHtHfSJdLFsxLDAsInxzKHApfCBcXGNvcHJvZCB8dChwKXwiXSxbMSwyLCJcXG1hdGhjYWx7TH1ee1xcYm90fSJdLFsyLDAsIlxcY29ybmVye1xcbWF0aGNhbCBQX3tcXG1hdGhjYWwgRn19Il0sWzIsMiwiXFxtYXRoY2Fse0d9Il0sWzEsMywibiJdLFsyLDBdLFswLDEsIlxcY2hpIiwyXSxbMywxXSxbMiw0XSxbNCw1XSxbMyw1XSxbMiwzXSxbMSwyLCIiLDEseyJzdHlsZSI6eyJuYW1lIjoiY29ybmVyIn19XSxbNSwyLCIiLDEseyJzdHlsZSI6eyJuYW1lIjoiY29ybmVyIn19XSxbNiwzXSxbNiwxXSxbNiw1XV0=
\begin{equation}
  \begin{tikzcd}
	{\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}}} & {|s(p)| \coprod |t(p)|} & {\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}}} \\
	\\
	{\mathcal{F}} & {\mathcal{D}} & {\mathcal{F}} \\
	& n
	\arrow["\chi_p"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=1-2, to=1-1]
	\arrow[from=1-2, to=1-3]
	\arrow[from=1-2, to=3-2]
	\arrow[from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=90}, draw=none, from=3-1, to=1-2]
	\arrow[from=3-2, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=3-2, to=3-3]
	\arrow["\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=180}, draw=none, from=3-3, to=1-2]
	\arrow[from=4-2, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=4-2, to=3-2]
	\arrow[from=4-2, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}~,
\tag{$\ast$}
\label{eq:outermost_box_decomposition_diagram_1}
\end{equation}
where we omitted internal interfaces for clarity.
The map $|s(p)| \coprod |t(p)| \xrightarrow{} \corner{\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{F}}}$ is mono, and therefore the corresponding complement given by $\mathcal{D}$ is uniquely determined by $\chi_p$.
Applying
\cref{lemma:root_edpoi_factorisation} gives a well-typed extended
cospan
\[
\mathcal D:u \coprod v\longrightarrow n
\]
such that
\[
\mathcal F
=
\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}}
\fatsemi\mathcal D.
\]

Now consider the original rewrite. Since its match is anchored inside
the component \(e\), preservation of the hierarchical relation implies
that every element in the image of the match lies inside \(e\).
Condition~(2) of the extended pushout complement further ensures that
every successor below a matched non-root edge also lies inside \(e\).
Hence neither \(p\), its boundary, nor any component \(d\neq e\) is
changed.

Restrict the two carrier pushout squares of the original EDPOI rewrite
to the sub-e-hypergraph rooted at \(e\). Pushouts in the present
construction are computed by taking disjoint unions of carrier sets
and quotienting by the boundary identifications. All non-boundary
elements affected by the rewrite lie inside \(e\), and all
identifications with the surrounding carrier occur through the
retained rule boundary. The restrictions are therefore again pushout
squares.

The retained interface maps restrict to the interfaces of
\(\mathcal F_e\). Conditions~(2) and~(3) are inherited from the
original match, and condition~(4) restricts to the corresponding
interface blocks. The comparison isomorphisms of condition~(5)
restrict to isomorphisms between the corresponding component blocks
and preserve their inherited orders. Hence the restricted diagram is
an EDPOI rewrite
\[
\mathcal F_e
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \langle\mathcal L,\mathcal R\rangle
}
\mathcal G_e
\]
for some well-typed extended cospan
\(\mathcal G_e:u\to v\).

Passing from \(\mathcal F\) to the component rooted at \(e\) removes
the enclosing box \(p\). The restricted match therefore has fewer
enclosing \(\boxsort\)-edges.

The original EDPOI construction changes only the component
\(\mathcal F_e\). Consequently, the distinguished box in the result
has component cospan \(\mathcal G_e\) at \(e\) and the old component
cospan \(\mathcal F_d\) at every \(d\neq e\). Thus its contents are
represented by
\[
\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}
=
\mathcal G_e
\join
\bigjoin_{\substack{
  d\in\operatorname{Comp}_{\mathcal F}(p)\\
  d\neq e
}}
\mathcal F_d.
\]

Consider now the rule $\langle \corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}},\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}}\rangle$ matched into $\mathcal{F}$ by $\chi_p$.
It induces an EDPOI diagram as
% https://q.uiver.app/#q=WzAsNyxbMCwwLCJcXGNvcm5lcntcXG1hdGhjYWwgUF97XFxtYXRoY2FsIEZ9fSJdLFswLDIsIlxcbWF0aGNhbHtGfSJdLFsxLDAsInxzKHApfCBcXGNvcHJvZCB8dChwKXwiXSxbMSwyLCJcXG1hdGhjYWx7TH1ee1xcYm90fSJdLFsyLDAsIlxcY29ybmVye1xcbWF0aGNhbCBQX3tcXG1hdGhjYWwgR319Il0sWzIsMiwiXFxtYXRoY2Fse0d9Il0sWzEsMywibiJdLFsyLDBdLFswLDEsIlxcY2hpIiwyXSxbMywxXSxbMiw0XSxbNCw1XSxbMyw1XSxbMiwzXSxbMSwyLCIiLDEseyJzdHlsZSI6eyJuYW1lIjoiY29ybmVyIn19XSxbNSwyLCIiLDEseyJzdHlsZSI6eyJuYW1lIjoiY29ybmVyIn19XSxbNiwzXSxbNiwxXSxbNiw1XV0=
\[\begin{tikzcd}
	{\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}}} & {|s(p)| \coprod |t(p)|} & {\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}}} \\
	\\
	{\mathcal{F}} & {\mathcal D} & {\mathcal{G}} \\
	& n
	\arrow["\chi_p"', from=1-1, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=1-2, to=1-1]
	\arrow[from=1-2, to=1-3]
	\arrow[from=1-2, to=3-2]
	\arrow[from=1-3, to=3-3]
	\arrow["\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=90}, draw=none, from=3-1, to=1-2]
	\arrow[from=3-2, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=3-2, to=3-3]
	\arrow["\lrcorner"{anchor=center, pos=0.125, rotate=180}, draw=none, from=3-3, to=1-2]
	\arrow[from=4-2, to=3-1]
	\arrow[from=4-2, to=3-2]
	\arrow[from=4-2, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}~.\]
Note that the left-hand side of the diagram is the same extended pushout complement as in~\eqref{eq:outermost_box_decomposition_diagram_1}.
Thus the second pushout replaces $\mathcal{F}_{e}$ with $\mathcal{G}_e$.
This gives us
\[
\mathcal G
=
\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}}
\fatsemi\mathcal D
\]
in \(\catname{EHypI}_{\bullet}(\Sigma)\).

Applying the quotient functor \(Q\) to the two factorisations and using
preservation of composition gives
\[
Q(\mathcal F)
=
Q(\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal F}})
\fatsemi Q(\mathcal D)
\]
and
\[
Q(\mathcal G)
=
Q(\corner{\mathcal P_{\mathcal G}})
\fatsemi Q(\mathcal D),
\]
as required.
\end{proof}

We now have both ingredients needed for the correspondence. 
Identity application and congruence translate a syntactic redex decomposition
into an EDPOI rewrite, while root factorisation and outermost-box
decomposition recover the corresponding syntactic decomposition from
an EDPOI match.

\subsection{Soundness and completeness}

\begin{theorem}[Soundness and completeness of EDPOI rewriting]
\label{theorem:edpoi_rewriting_frob}
Let \(a,b:m\to n\) and \(l,r:i\to j\) be raw
\(\Sigma^{\join}\)-terms. Then
\[
[a]\Rightarrow_{\langle[l],[r]\rangle}[b]
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner a)
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
  \left\langle
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner l),
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)
  \right\rangle
}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner b).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We first prove the forward implication by induction on the derivation of
\[
[a]\Rightarrow_{\langle[l],[r]\rangle}[b]
\]
in~\cref{def:enriched_prop_rewriting}.

Suppose first that the rewrite is witnessed by a decomposition of the following form:
 \[
\adjustbox{scale=0.7}{
\tikzfig{./figures/string/decomposition}
}~.
\] 
Applying $\cup$ to the outer context as in~\cref{theorem:dpoi_rewriting_frob},
produces a morphism
\[
d:i+j\longrightarrow m+n
\]
of \(\mathbf H^{\join}_\Sigma\) such that
\begin{equation}
\corner{[a]}
=
\corner{[l]}\fatsemi d,
\qquad
\corner{[b]}
=
\corner{[r]}\fatsemi d.
\tag{1}
\label{eq:edpoi_rewriting_frob_1}
\end{equation}

Choose a raw term
\[
\delta:i+j\longrightarrow m+n
\]
representing \(d\). By
\cref{lemma:edpoi_identity_application} and
\cref{lemma:rewriting_congruence}, there is an EDPOI rewrite
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner l)\fatsemi\mathrm{Rep}(\delta)
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}_{
  \left\langle
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner l),
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)
  \right\rangle
}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)\fatsemi\mathrm{Rep}(\delta).
\tag{2}
\label{eq:edpoi_rewriting_frob_2}
\end{equation}
By~\cref{lemma:concrete_interpretation_quotient},
\cref{lemma:concrete_interpretation_bending}, and~\eqref{eq:edpoi_rewriting_frob_1},
\[
\begin{aligned}
Q(\mathrm{Rep}(\corner a))
&=I(\corner{[a]})\\
&=I(\corner{[l]}\fatsemi d)\\
&=
Q\bigl(
  \mathrm{Rep}(\corner l)\fatsemi\mathrm{Rep}(\delta)
\bigr).
\end{aligned}
\]
Hence
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner a)
\sim_{\mathfrak R}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner l)\fatsemi\mathrm{Rep}(\delta).
\]
Similarly,
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)\fatsemi\mathrm{Rep}(\delta)
\sim_{\mathfrak R}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner b).
\]
Together with \eqref{eq:edpoi_rewriting_frob_2}, these are the witnesses required by
\cref{def:edpoi_modulo_structural}.

Suppose now that the decomposition is witnessed by:
\begin{equation*}
\adjustbox{scale=0.7}{
\tikzfig{./figures/monoidal_egraphs/enriched_decomposition}~.
}
\end{equation*}

and consider the smaller rewrite
\[
[a']\Rightarrow_{\langle[l],[r]\rangle}[b'].
\]
Let \(\gamma\) denote the join of the
unchanged components. Bending the common outer context gives a
morphism \(d\) satisfying
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\corner{[a]}
&=
\bigl(
  \corner{[a']}\join\corner\gamma
\bigr)\fatsemi d,\\
\corner{[b]}
&=
\bigl(
  \corner{[b']}\join\corner\gamma
\bigr)\fatsemi d.
\end{aligned}
\tag{3}
\label{eq:edpoi_rewriting_frob_4}
\end{equation}
% Here bending distributes over the join by
% \cref{lemma:concrete_interpretation_bending}.

By the induction hypothesis,
\[
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner a')
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
  \left\langle
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner l),
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)
  \right\rangle
}
\mathrm{Rep}(\corner b').
\]
Choose raw terms representing \(\gamma\) and \(d\), say $\gamma'$ and $d'$ such that 
\[
[\gamma'] = \gamma, \qquad [d'] = d.
\]
Applying
\cref{lemma:rewriting_modulo_congruence} twice gives 
\[
(\mathrm{Rep}(\corner a') \join \mathrm{Rep}(\corner\gamma')) \fatsemi \mathrm{Rep}(d')
\;\overline{\rightsquigarrow}^{\,\mathfrak R}_{
  \left\langle
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner l),
    \mathrm{Rep}(\corner r)
  \right\rangle
}
(\mathrm{Rep}(\corner b') \join \mathrm{Rep}(\corner \gamma')) \fatsemi \mathrm{Rep}(d').
\]
By~\eqref{eq:edpoi_rewriting_frob_4} and
\cref{lemma:term_equations_realised}, the resulting endpoints are
structurally equivalent to
\(\mathrm{Rep}(\corner a)\) and
\(\mathrm{Rep}(\corner b)\). 
This proves the forward implication.

For the converse, we first establish the following claim. Let
\[
f,g:m\to n,
